# Archive: archive

## archive/README.md
```
# Form King Historical Data Archive

This archive is a **point-in-time snapshot** of Form King B2C meeting, speedmap, and race-form data for the date range, states, and benchmark depth you purchased. It is not live data.

## Contents

| Path | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `meetings/{meetingId}/summary.json` | Meeting summary (same shape as `GET /b2c/meetings/{meetingId}`) |
| `meetings/{meetingId}/speedmaps.json` | Meeting speedmaps (same shape as `GET /b2c/meetings/{meetingId}/speedmaps`) |
| `meetings/{meetingId}/races/{raceId}.json` | Race form (same shape as `GET /b2c/meetings/{meetingId}/races/{raceId}`) |
| `days/{ddmmyy}.json` | Meetings for that day (same shape as `GET /b2c/meetings/date/{ddmmyy}`); `[]` when there was no archived racing |
| `index.json` | Walking aid: every day, meeting and race id in the archive (not an API route) |
| `fk_historical_proxy.py` | Local HTTP proxy so existing API clients can call `localhost` |
| `run_proxy.command` | Double-click launcher for the proxy on **macOS** |
| `run_proxy.bat` | Double-click launcher for the proxy on **Windows** |
| `license.md` | Archive coverage (range, states, benchmarks, …) and licence terms; links to https://www.formking.com.au/api-terms |
| `api-terms.html` | Snapshot of the Form King API Terms current when the archive was generated (if it could be captured) |
| `errors.md` | Optional build errors / warnings |

## Unzip first

The download is an **AES password-protected** zip. This proxy does **not** unzip it.

1. Use the password from the admin `202` response body (or a later progress poll) when the archive was requested.
2. Unzip to a local folder (any tool that supports AES zip encryption, e.g. `unzip` on macOS/Linux with a compatible build, 7-Zip on Windows, etc.).
3. You should see `meetings/`, `fk_historical_proxy.py`, `run_proxy.command`, `run_proxy.bat`, and this `README.md` at the root of the unpacked folder.

## Run the local proxy

### Easiest: double-click

- **macOS**: double-click `run_proxy.command`. If macOS warns the file is from an unidentified developer, right-click it and choose **Open** instead, then confirm.
- **Windows**: double-click `run_proxy.bat`.

Either launcher checks for Python, prints a friendly message with a download link if it's missing, and otherwise starts the proxy and keeps the window open so you can see the log / stop it with Ctrl+C (or by closing the window).

### Manual: command line

Requires **Python 3.11+**. No pip packages — stdlib only.

```bash
cd /path/to/unpacked-archive
python fk_historical_proxy.py --port 8080
```

Optional: `python fk_historical_proxy.py --port 8080 --dir /path/to/unpacked-archive`

You should see: `Serving historical data archive on http://127.0.0.1:8080 ...`

### Installing Python (if you don't have it)

- **Windows**: download the installer from https://www.python.org/downloads/windows/ and make sure you tick **"Add python.exe to PATH"** during setup.
- **macOS**: download the installer from https://www.python.org/downloads/macos/, or if you use Homebrew: `brew install python3`.
- Either way, Python 3.11 or newer is recommended; the proxy uses only the standard library, so no further `pip install` steps are needed.

## Example URLs

Once the proxy is running:

- `http://localhost:8080/b2c/meetings/date/{ddmmyy}` (e.g. `010725`; optional `?states=VIC,NSW`)
- `http://localhost:8080/b2c/meetings/{meetingId}`
- `http://localhost:8080/b2c/meetings/{meetingId}/speedmaps`
- `http://localhost:8080/b2c/meetings/{meetingId}/speedmaps/{raceId}`
- `http://localhost:8080/b2c/meetings/{meetingId}/races/{raceId}`

Auth headers (`x-api-key`) are ignored — the archive was the paid product. No credits are deducted.

The proxy serves only the live B2C routes above, with the same response shapes and error bodies as the live API (400 `Invalid date. Expected DDMMYY format`, 404 `{"message":"Not found"}`). A valid day with no archived racing returns `200 []`.

## Walking the archive

Open `index.json` to see which days, meetings and races the archive holds:

```json
{"apiVersion": "1.1.0", "from": "010725", "to": "020725", "states": ["VIC"],
 "days": [{"date": "010725", "meetings": [{"meetingId": "...", "state": "VIC", "raceIds": ["..."]}]}]}
```

Then call the live routes with those ids:

```bash
curl http://localhost:8080/b2c/meetings/date/010725
curl http://localhost:8080/b2c/meetings/{meetingId}
curl http://localhost:8080/b2c/meetings/{meetingId}/speedmaps
curl http://localhost:8080/b2c/meetings/{meetingId}/races/{raceId}
```

```python
import json
from urllib.request import urlopen

base = "http://localhost:8080"
index = json.load(open("index.json"))
for day in index["days"]:
    for meeting in day["meetings"]:
        for race_id in meeting["raceIds"]:
            url = f"{base}/b2c/meetings/{meeting['meetingId']}/races/{race_id}"
            race = json.load(urlopen(url))
            print(day["date"], meeting["meetingId"], race_id)
```

Older archives may lack `days/` and `index.json`; the proxy then rebuilds the date route from `meetings/*/summary.json`.

## Snapshot validity

This data is valid only for the originally requested date range, states, and benchmark depth stamped in `license.md`. Re-fetch from the live API if you need current form.

```
